# OS Development Roadmap — SIX-AGENT EDITION
## Phase 3: Graphics and GUI
### 3.5 GUI toolkit / widget API

- [x] Layout engine (Flexbox/Grid-based, not CSS — native implementation)
  - [x] FlexDirection (Row/Column), FlexWrap, FlexJustify (Start/End/Center/SpaceBetween/SpaceAround/SpaceEvenly)
  - [x] FlexAlign (Start/End/Center/Stretch/Baseline), gap support
  - [x] FlexItem with grow/shrink/basis, per-item align override
  - [x] LayoutBox output with position, size, children
- [x] Styling system (subset of CSS properties without cascade complexity)
  - [x] Style struct: width/height/min/max, padding/margin (Edges), borders, corner radii
  - [x] Background/foreground colors, font size/weight, text alignment, cursor
  - [x] BoxShadow, opacity, overflow (visible/hidden/scroll)
- [x] Signals and slots (signal.rs: Signal<T>, connect/emit/disconnect, re-entrancy safe, EventBus named registry, SignalGroup)
- [x] Core widgets:
  - [x] Buttons (text), labels
  - [x] Menu bar (menubar.rs: top-level labels, dropdown, hot-tracking, submenus, mnemonics, accelerators)
  - [x] Input fields (single-line TextInput, multiline TextArea)
  - [x] Checkboxes, tristate checkboxes (Unchecked/Checked/Indeterminate)
  - [x] Radio buttons
  - [x] Treeview (tree.rs: expand/collapse, selection, keyboard nav, connection lines, scroll)
  - [x] Tabs view (tabs.rs: close buttons, dirty indicator, overflow scroll, Ctrl+Tab nav)
  - [x] Scroll bars (ScrollView widget)
  - [x] Context menus (menu.rs: Action/Separator/Submenu, keyboard nav, edge-flip, checkmarks)
  - [x] Tooltips (menu.rs: hover delay, auto-position, max-width wrap)
  - [x] Color picker (colorpicker.rs: HSV square+hue bar, RGB/HSV sliders, hex input, alpha, 48 presets, eyedropper, recent colors)
  - [x] Grid view (grid.rs: virtual-scrolled grid, icon+label cells, multi-select, rubber-band, type-ahead, drag)
  - [x] Modal and non-modal dialogs, alert popups (modal.rs: ModalOverlay, AlertDialog, InputDialog, ProgressDialog, NonModalDialog)
- [x] Render tree abstraction (backend-agnostic drawing primitives)
  - [x] RenderCommand enum: FillRect, StrokeRect, Text, Image, Line, BoxShadow
  - [x] Clip/translate stack (PushClip/PopClip, PushTranslate/PopTranslate)
  - [x] RenderTree with command list and viewport
- [x] Additional widgets: Separator, ProgressBar, Slider, Image, Container

  - [x] SGR colors (8-color, 256-color, truecolor), bold/dim/italic/underline/blink/inverse

  - [x] ~~PTY integration with child process (pty.rs: PtyPair, ByteChannel, cooked/raw mode, line discipline, PtyManager, ChildProcess)~~ — **struck 2026-09-24 (lane E): `pty.rs` modelled a pseudo-terminal inside the terminal's own process, and no child ever ran on it** — its `ChildProcess` was a counter, and the shell attached later ran on pipes. Replaced: the child is the user's shell on a kernel pseudo-terminal through `libcall::pty` (`apps/terminal/src/child.rs`); see lane E's backlog entry in "Current backlog by lane".
  - [x] **Windowed** (apps/terminal: a grid that is a quotient of the live window size, a scrollback bar with a thumb that says where in the history the viewport is, selection and copy expressed in buffer rows, a real PTY under it, and a bell and a blink that age on a clock; 124 tests). This is the forty-fifth application in the wiring campaign and the one where the gap between what was written and what ran was widest: there was a working VT parser, and beside it in the same crate a complete two-thousand-line pseudo-terminal with a line discipline, cooked and raw modes, a window size and a child process, and **the two halves had never been introduced** -- `pty.rs` had no caller at all outside its own tests. Wiring it found eleven faults on top of the missing window. (1) `main` fed the terminal two strings, called `render`, bound the result to `let _commands` and returned: the frame was rendered into a `Vec` and dropped on the next line, so nothing was ever shown and no key ever reached anything. (2) **There was no child.** Not a slow one or a mis-plumbed one -- none. Nothing opened a PTY, nothing wrote a keystroke to one, nothing read one back; the terminal was a VT parser having a private conversation with itself. It opens a `PtyPair` now, a key goes out through it and what the child writes comes back on the tick. (3) **The grid was eighty by twenty-four whatever the window was.** `resize` existed and had no caller outside the tests, so a maximised terminal drew a small rectangle of text in the corner of a large empty window, and a narrow one drew its right-hand columns past the edge onto the desktop. `Layout::solve` derives the grid from the live size on the resize event *and* on every frame. (4) **`PtyMaster::resize` -- this tree's `TIOCSWINSZ` -- had no caller either**, so a shell under this terminal wrapped its prompt at eighty columns in a window twice that wide. The child is told the new size whenever the grid changes. (5) **The selection copied the wrong line.** It recorded a *screen* row and read `self.screen` with that number, while the drawing pass walked the scrollback: selecting a line of history highlighted the right text and copied whatever happened to be at that screen slot. Selection, highlight and copy all go through `buffer_row_of`/`line_at` now, so they cannot disagree about which line row zero is. (6) **A line falling off the front of the scrollback dragged the selection with it.** Buffer rows index a run whose front moves, which is the one thing an index cannot survive on its own: a selection made a thousand lines ago crept down the buffer as output arrived, silently, since every row involved stayed in range. `forget_oldest_row` walks the selection back by one when the oldest line is dropped. (7) **The visual bell was a countdown of frames**, so it lasted a tenth of a second at sixty frames a second, twice that at thirty, and *for ever* on a terminal with nothing else to redraw for -- which is the usual state of a terminal waiting at a prompt. It ages by the elapsed milliseconds the clock reports, and `frame` takes `&self` so a drawing pass cannot age anything at all. (8) **`config.cursor_blink` was a setting nothing read**: the cursor was painted on every frame regardless. The blink runs on the same clock, is restarted whenever the user types or the child writes, and a cursor whose blink is turned off mid-blink is not left in the dark half of it. (9) **There was no scrollbar and no other sign that scrollback existed** -- a terminal scrolled a thousand lines back looked exactly like one sitting at a prompt with a quiet child, and the only way to find out which was to press a key. A bar is reserved down the right edge (never more than a quarter of the window, so a narrow window is still mostly terminal), its thumb has a four-pixel floor so a ten-thousand-line history does not give it a quarter of a pixel, a press in it pages towards where it landed, and a press in it does not start a selection -- the bar sits over the last column, where a drag would otherwise select text. (10) **`parse_extended_color` range-checked one of three components and then indexed all three**, and narrowed with `as u8`, which wraps: `38;5;256` painted black while meaning white. All three are fetched together and the narrowing saturates. (11) **Fifty-seven lints were hidden behind crate-level allows**, including the `as` casts behind fault 10; all of them are gone, fixed rather than suppressed. Two more faults surfaced while the tests were being written, and both are lesson 102: **`output_buffer` had two writers and one dead end** -- keystrokes went straight to the PTY while the parser's own replies (the cursor position report, the device attributes answer) were queued and drained by nothing, so a full-screen program that asked this terminal where its cursor was waited for an answer sitting in a `Vec`; and `on_event` **re-appended the bytes `handle_event` had just queued**, so every character typed arrived doubled. `to_child` only queues now and `flush_to_child` is the one route out. Worse, **`on_event` answered a tick itself** -- it needed to know whether anything had changed before it could say whether the frame was worth drawing -- and returned before the dispatch that reads the child could run, so under a real window, the only place `on_event` is called, the terminal was write-only: the shell's prompt was produced and never collected. Every test that caught the read called `handle_event` directly, which is the path the compositor does not take. The suite sweeps a 48-point grid of window sizes for every layout invariant (lesson 97) and draws thirteen states rather than one for every whole-frame claim (lesson 99), and the grid's hit box is exempted from the "ink at exactly that rectangle" rule by a *second* test rather than by weakening the first (lesson 100), because the grid is a band of many cells of which most are the default background and are painted not at all. The mutation table is 58 rows and the first sweep caught 48 of the 59 it then held; every one of the eleven that did not was a fault in the tests or in the table, and none was answered by weakening an assertion. **Four were tests that could not see the thing they were named for** -- two asked the decider instead of the picture (`is_selected` directly, and the `blink_on` field, so the drawing pass was free to consult the wrong row or not consult the field at all), one supplied a bound the code no longer declared (`max_width.unwrap_or_else(measure)`: one character measured is one cell wide, so an unbounded glyph looked bounded), and one never filled the PTY channel, so a short write that dropped its remainder looked identical to a whole one. Those are lesson 103, and cost three new tests and two strengthenings. **One test was satisfied by any injective map** -- a scroll round trip says the offset means the same thing in both directions but not which end it counts from, so a viewport top counted forward from the oldest line passed it; it now pins that the live view is the newest screenful. **Three rows named the wrong owning test**, which is a claim about coverage that was simply false: the grid-quotient row's second test measures the grid's hit box against a band that *is* the grid, so it moves with the mutation; the exact-cell-count row's rounding drift is invisible at one or two cells (`8.4f32 + 8.4f32` is exactly `16.8f32`) and shows only across the eighty columns the window opens at; and the thumb-hit-box row is not caught by the ink test, because the track is a fill at precisely that rectangle -- what the mutation destroys is the thumb's *position*. **Two rows mutated guards that cannot change any picture at any size** -- the frame's outer clip, and the intersection the grid's hit box is taken from -- because every rectangle the pass paints is derived from the window it is being clipped to, and `draw_cells` already caps the rows and columns it walks. They were removed with the reason recorded in the table rather than given an owner, which would have been a lie about what the suite checks. **And one row was mis-modelled in a way that hid a real fault:** it made `to_child` write straight to the PTY and expected the reply test to fail -- but the parser's replies were bypassing `to_child` entirely at all three sites, so the row was asking a question about a funnel two of whose inlets were not connected to it. The repair was to connect them; the row now blanks the cursor position report itself.

- [x] Lock screen (apps/lockscreen: clock/date display, password entry, shake animation, attempt tracking with progressive lockout, multi-user support). Reworked 2026-08-24 so it can actually unlock: it no longer checks the password itself but asks a `PasswordAuthority`, which returns the same six-case `AuthOutcome` as lane B's `authlib::Outcome` (Accepted/Rejected/Locked/NoPassword/Unusable/RateLimited), and an acceptance raises a one-shot unlock ticket the host collects via `take_unlock_request` — spent once, revoked by a later wrong guess or a user switch. Previously the answer was discarded, so a green suite covered a screen that could never unlock (`known-issues.md` → `TD-C-THE-LOCK-SCREEN-THROWS-AWAY-THE-ANSWER-TO-THE-ONLY-QUESTION-IT-ASKS`, now RESOLVED); rationale in `design-decisions.md` §545. 78 tests, proved against 19 reintroduced defects by `scripts/reintro-lockscreen.py`. The passwordless-account policy is preserved as-is and filed in `open-questions.md`; the real `logind` client replaces `PasswordValidator` once lane A lands caller identity (`requests/b-a-a-service-cannot-find-out-who-is-calling-it.md`). *2026-09-27 (lane E): whether the clock shows seconds and the date is the user's -- Settings' Screen Lock page sets it, `lockscreen.yaml` keeps it, the lock screen reads it (C-Q26, §1418). It was compiled in and the same for everyone.*
- [x] Disk cleanup utility (apps/diskcleanup: 9 cleanup categories, scanner, executor with dry-run, scheduled cleanup, history, progress UI)
  - [x] `[E]` **It looks in your home, and at SlateOS's recycle bin** (2026-09-27) -- the recycle bin, thumbnail and browser categories looked in `home/user` for everyone, the bin in another desktop's `~/.local/share/Trash` and the thumbnails in a `~/.cache/thumbnails` nothing writes, so each found nothing to free. They use `HOME` now (a home that cannot be placed is not guessed), the bin is `~/.recycle` read through `recyclebin` -- one item per recycled thing, shown by the name it was deleted under -- and the thumbnails are `gui/thumbs`' `~/.cache/thumbs`. `apps/diskcleanup/mutate.py`.
- [x] Emoji picker (apps/emojipicker: **windowed** — category tabs, search, responsive emoji grid, skin tone selector, 82 emoji, recent tracking, 85 tests)
  - Opens a real window via `oswindow::app::launch`. Every clickable thing is a `Target` whose box is recorded by the renderer as it paints, so the cell a user sees and the cell a click resolves to are one frame rather than two expressions kept equal by hand: `grid_hit_test` used to re-derive the grid from the same constants `render_grid` drew it from.
  - Fixed the fixed geometry the wiring exposed. `WINDOW_WIDTH` and `WINDOW_HEIGHT` appeared in nineteen expressions, so the picker drew a 360x480 popup into whatever window it was given — uncaught because there was no window: `main` rendered one tree, took its length, and exited. Column count, band positions and the centring gutter now come from a `Layout` computed from the live window size and never remembered, so a wider window is more columns rather than a wider margin.
  - A window too short for everything drops whole bands rather than crushing the grid, and their hit boxes go with them: the preview is sacrificed first (it names an emoji already visible a row above), then the search row, then the skin-tone strip, which is the only place a tone can be chosen at all.
  - An emoji scrolled out of the viewport is no longer clickable. The arithmetic hit test subtracted the scroll offset and divided, so nothing in the calculation knew about the viewport; recording hit boxes inside the clip drops them instead.
- [x] Font manager (apps/fontmanager: category sidebar, font list, preview panel, install/uninstall, rendering settings, 19 default fonts)
- [x] Task scheduler (apps/taskscheduler: **windowed** — `app::launch`, `Frame` hit boxes, `Layout` from the live size, a once-a-second tick that runs whatever became due; cron expression parsing, retry policies, execution history, task CRUD, config persistence, 128 tests. No way to start a process yet — held open by a `RunFn` seam that leaves a due task overdue rather than rescheduling one it could not run, see `known-issues.md` → `C-TASKSCHEDULER-HAS-NO-EXECUTOR`. **Second app through the `C-CENTRING-IS-NOT-A-BOUND` sweep**, and the one that showed what the campaign's recipe was still missing. The two containment tests went in first — `nothing_is_painted_outside_the_window`, which checks fills and strokes only because `frame()` clips `layout.content` and a clip hides an overrun from text and hit boxes but not from ink (lesson 107), and `no_pass_paints_outside_the_region_it_owns`, which hands each sub-pass an *unclipped* frame and checks all three witnesses — over an eight-by-eight size grid with a sliver rung and eleven app states. The first mutation sweep was 21 of 29, and **the eight survivors fell into three shapes, none of which the campaign's recipe had named**. One was a **bound nothing could squeeze**: both list bodies were inline loop blocks, and the only rectangle they ever saw came from `scroll_window::visible`, which counts *whole* rows — so the row's `intersect`, the checkbox's clamp and all five cells' `run_in` were exercised at exactly one height and could be deleted without a test noticing. A correctly-bounded body whose loop only ever produces one size is dead code as far as the suite is concerned; the fix is structural, not additive — `draw_task_row` and `draw_history_row` are functions *of the row* now, which is what lets `squeezes()` hand them a three-point one, and the column offsets they share with the heading strip moved to module scope (`COL_*_X`) so two functions cannot drift into a table that lies about what it is showing. That extraction immediately found the app's one real geometry bug: a row squeezed to nothing **still painted its background and still recorded a `TaskRow` hit box** — a click target for a task the user cannot see. Three more survivors were **clamps a stronger guard already dominated**: `CHECKBOX_SIZE.min(row.h)` under an `.intersect(row)` that cuts the same rectangle, and two caret clamps under a `centre_line`/`text_span` pair that had already refused any field small enough to make them bind. A clamp no input can make bind is not a guard, it is a claim the tests cannot check, so all three were deleted and their mutation rows re-pointed at the bound that does the work — never at a weakened assertion. The remaining four were the campaign's blind spot: **containment can always be satisfied by drawing nothing.** Every bound here is an intersection and an intersection is equally happy to return empty, so a zero-area fill is inside every region there is — which is why deleting a control's "no area, no hit box" guard, or hanging a dialog's buttons off its nominal 380-point height so the cut removes them entirely, left every containment assertion satisfied. Two assertions close that: each pass handed an empty box must produce no ink *and* no hit box, and `a_dialog_with_room_still_shows_its_buttons` checks the converse — a dialog cut down to fit must still have its Save and Cancel. 131 tests, 30 mutation rows, all caught by the test named for each (lesson 109))

Since 2026-09-25 it lists, opens, marks and trims recordings already on disk*

- [x] PDF viewer (apps/pdfviewer: **windowed** — `app::launch`, `Frame` hit boxes, `Layout` from the live size; multi-tab, bookmarks, search, zoom modes, page rotation, print settings, dark mode, 183 tests. **"annotations" was listed here and removed 2026-09-18: nothing can create one.** `add_highlight`, `add_note` and `add_freehand` have no production caller, and they are the only callers of `add_annotation`, which holds the only `annotations.push` -- so the chain is dead at the top. The module doc is the honest version and says "Annotation support **model**", which claims a data model rather than a feature; this line dropped the word. **Dark mode only became true on the same day** -- `dark_mode` was `true` at construction with no writer, so every page rendered inverted until `D` was bound. No PDF parser and no print spooler yet — held open by `OpenFn`/`PrintFn` seams, see `known-issues.md` → `C-PDFVIEWER-HAS-NO-PDF-PARSER-AND-NO-PRINT-SPOOLER`)
- [x] Device manager (apps/devicemanager: device tree with 8 categories, driver info, resource view, search/filter, hardware report export, 105 tests)
- [x] Alarm clock (apps/alarmclock: alarms with repeat/snooze, multiple timers, stopwatch with laps and stats, progress ring, AM/PM+24h, 180 tests)
  - [x] **Windowed** (apps/alarmclock: `app::launch`, a `Layout` solved from the live window size every frame, and hit boxes recorded by the drawing pass). `fn main` was empty, which for this program in particular is not a missing feature but the absence of the one thing it does: nothing delivered `Event::Tick`, so a countdown a user started could never reach zero, a snooze never expired, and the clock face read whatever the wall clock said when the process started. Wiring it gave every control a `Target`, converted the three per-item `render() -> Vec<RenderCommand>` helpers to `draw(&mut Frame, ..)` passes that record their own hit boxes as they draw them, held the alarm editor apart from the alarm it edits so Cancel really cancels, and gave `impl App` a `tick_interval` that is never `None` — 50 ms while the stopwatch shows hundredths, 500 ms otherwise, with the per-second work banked off the *measured* `elapsed_ms` so a one-minute timer takes one minute at either rate. It also panic-proofed arithmetic the file had carried unchecked (`minutes_until`, the progress ring's segment ends, the lap mean), which the clippy gate reaches only once the crate is actually built. The geometry sweeps then found four more, none of which any of the 175 tests that existed at the time could see. (1) **All three scrolling panes — alarms, timers, the stopwatch's lap table — drew every item they held and relied on the clip to hide the rest.** Twenty alarms in a pane with room for four meant sixteen cards painted where nothing could ever show them, unbounded in all three collections and reachable at the app's own opening size. Each loop now tests the item's own edges against the pane and `break`s at the first one past the bottom. (2) **The file's `text` helper pushed a `RenderCommand::Text` unconditionally where `guitk::put_text` asks `Frame::is_visible` first**, so a lap row four hundred points below the table was a command in the frame claiming to be a label that is present — ink and hit boxes governed by two different rules, which is the half of lesson 107 that bites hardest, since `Frame::hit` had been dropping those same rows' hit boxes all along. (3) **`text_centred` declared its `max_width` from where the run *starts* rather than from the box it was told to centre in**, so every centred run in the file was declared to overhang its box by half the slack: "Stopwatch" in a 120-point tab was declared to run to 385 in a 360-point window, and because the renderer trims at the bound it is given, there was no ellipsis either. (4) **The alarm editor laid its stack out at a fixed 308 points against a content area 248 points tall at this app's own minimum window size**, so Save and Cancel were painted below the panel, hidden by the clip with their hit boxes dropped — and since the editor covers the alarm tab, that was a trap the pointer could not leave. The stack is solved for the height it was given now, guarding NaN explicitly rather than through `clamp`, which propagates it into every rectangle below where it compares false against every edge and escapes every bound. The suite is 180 tests, three of them the geometry sweeps, and `the_editor_can_always_be_left_by_pointer` asks through `frame` at ten window sizes and clicks Cancel at each. The mutation table is 34 rows.
- [x] Unit converter (apps/unitconverter: 12 categories, conversions through base unit, affine temperature handling, history, favorites)
- [x] System restore manager (apps/systemrestore: snapshot tree, create/restore/compare, scheduling, retention policies, timeline view, export/import, storage management) — *as built (2026-09-27): real restore points of every program's settings and data (`~/.config/slateos`), kept in `apps/snapstore`; restore makes the folder match after keeping it as a "Before restoring" point; the system's own files are shown and not offered (§1217, E-Q3). It showed five invented points until then.*
- [x] System restore (apps/systemrestore: tree-based snapshots, scheduling, retention, comparison, export/import, storage management)
- [x] Unit converter (apps/unitconverter: 12 categories, base-unit conversion, history, favorites, formula display)
- [x] Partition manager (apps/partmanager: visual disk map, GPT/MBR, create/delete/resize/format with pending queue, SMART health, multi-disk sidebar, 101 tests)
- [x] Credential manager (apps/credmanager: password vault, login/note/card/identity/SSH entries, generator with entropy, audit, folders/tags, auto-lock, 122 tests) — *as built (2026-09-27): the vault is kept on disk at last -- `<config>/credmanager/vault`, sealed with XChaCha20-Poly1305 under an Argon2id key from the master password (`rustcrypto/seal`, §1218); a first run makes it from a master password typed twice; locking forgets the key and every entry; every change is saved under a new nonce. Until then it kept nothing. Back up writes the vault sealed (it opens with the master password and restores everything); Restore opens a backup with its master password and asks before replacing; Export writes plain-text CSV, every field quoted, only after a warning that says what the file is (C-Q25, §1417).*
- [x] VPN manager (apps/vpnmanager: OpenVPN/WireGuard/IPSec/L2TP/PPTP/SSTP protocols, kill switch, split tunneling, connection logs, profile import/export, DNS override, 135 tests)
- [x] Contacts/address book (apps/contacts: vCard 3.0 import/export, groups with colors, favorites, birthday tracking, duplicate detection with merge, multi-field search, social accounts, 234 tests)
  - [x] **Windowed** (apps/contacts: `app::launch`, a `Layout` solved from the live window size every frame, and hit boxes recorded by the drawing pass). This is the forty-eighth application in the wiring campaign. `main` built a store, loaded the sample data, drew one frame into a `Vec` and dropped it, so nobody saw the picture and nothing could be pressed. Wiring it found six faults, and the sweeps found seven more. (1) **The drawing pass recorded no hit boxes at all**, so every control in the program — the `+` button, the search bar, the filter and sort cells, the A-Z rail, the contact rows, Edit, Star, Delete, Call, Email, Map, the fifteen form fields, Save, Cancel and Merge — was a picture of a control. (2) **The Groups and Duplicates panels were unreachable.** Both were fully written and fully drawn, and the only way to select either was to assign to `self.view` from code. (3) **A fifteen-field form had a two-field `Tab` cycle**: `FormField::next` named the second field's successor explicitly instead of wrapping over `ALL`, so thirteen fields could not be typed into by any route. (4) **Sixteen `#[allow(dead_code)]` attributes** covered the file, five of them hiding palette entries nothing used. (5) **Nineteen runs of text were drawn with `max_width: None`**, so a long company name walked over the column beside it and off the edge of the panel; every run goes through one `put_text` funnel now. (6) **The layout was `SIDEBAR_WIDTH` and `self.window_height` written into the commands** — the sidebar could not shrink, and the height was the size the app *believed* it was, which after a resize is the size it used to be. Found by the size sweep: **the status strip was placed after the header and pushed down to clear it**, so a thirty-pixel window drew "Ready" below its own bottom edge; **text was emitted with no visibility check**, so a 40x30 window drew "Contacts" bounded to a width of zero; and **the visibility test asked about the row a run sits in rather than the run itself**, so a star centred in a visible row was drawn four pixels below anything that could show it. Found by the interaction sweep: **a merge reported "Merged into #7"**, the id of a contact the merge had just invented and the user had never seen. The suite is 234 tests, of which 14 sweep 12 window sizes across 9 states, and the mutation table is 37 rows. **The last two faults were found by the mutation sweep and were invisible to all 230 tests that existed at the time**, which is lesson 107: `draw_contact_fields` walked a cursor down the detail panel without ever asking whether it still had panel left, so a contact with six phone numbers and a paragraph of notes painted its group chips *on top of* the Edit / Star / Delete buttons at 640x480, and `draw_edit_form` did the same to Save and Cancel at 1024x768. A clip hides an overrun from two of the three kinds of thing a frame holds — `put_text` declines to emit a run the clip cannot show, and `Frame::hit` trims a hit box to nothing — but a *fill* is pushed exactly as asked, so fills are the only surface that assertion can be phrased over. Of the first sweep's seven non-`[ok]` verdicts, four were mis-aimed expectations and three were blind spots: a probe aimed at a view cell cannot see a resize, because the cells barely move between 1024 and 600 wide (the A-Z rail, pinned to the right edge of a fractional sidebar, can); a fresh app's form is already empty, so "the form opens carrying the last contact's text" had to dirty it first; and the panel-versus-sidebar width rule breaks only in a 44-pixel band between the panel's minimum and the sidebar's cap, which a twelve-size grid stepped straight over — that test sweeps every width now.

- [x] RSS reader (apps/rssreader: Atom/RSS feed parser, article management, OPML import/export, feed categories, unread tracking, search, multi-panel UI)
- [x] Calendar (apps/calendar: **windowed** — month/week/day/year/agenda views, ICS import/export, recurring events with 7 patterns, reminders, 10 event categories, mini-calendar sidebar, 76 tests) — *2026-09-27 (lane E): the events, the file they are kept in and iCalendar are their own crate, `apps/calendarstore`, so the desktop's calendar popup can read the same file (lane C, C-Q19; §1219). Mutation sweep: 67 rows across both crates, all caught.*
  - Opens a real window via `oswindow::app::launch` and is driven by events. It had no click handling at all — `main` rendered each of the five views, counted the commands and exited — and three features were dead in consequence, not by oversight but because nothing could reach them: the category filter only recoloured its own sidebar swatch (every view called `store.events_on` directly), `search_results` was computed by `search()` and read by nothing, and `selected_event_id` was never read anywhere in the file. All three are now wired to the thing that was missing, a pointer.
  - Every clickable thing is a `Target` whose box is recorded by the renderer as it paints it, so the day a user sees and the day a click resolves to are one frame rather than two expressions kept equal by hand. An event chip drawn over a day cell takes the click off the cell for free, because the later box wins.
  - Geometry now comes from a `Layout` computed from the live window size and never remembered. The chrome is allocated right-to-left: the view tabs first — they are the only way to change view, so they shrink their pitch rather than run off the edge — then the search box, then the caption, which is dropped first because it only names a view already on screen. The sidebar goes when the content area could not spare it.
  - Week and day views draw 1192px and 1476px of hour grid. In a 720px window everything after early afternoon was below the bottom edge with no way to reach it, which nobody noticed because there was no window and no wheel. The content area scrolls, the wheel goes through `wheel::pixels` (its `dy` is a notch count, not a distance), and the wheel over the sidebar steps the mini calendar's month instead.
  - `main` opened on a hardcoded `2026-05-18`, so every "today" highlight pointed at a fixed day. It now reads the clock, and a one-minute tick moves it across midnight.
  - The month-grid start offset was written out three times as the same `if first_dow == 0 { 6 } else { first_dow - 1 }`; it is now one method. Fixed the crate's ten pre-existing `arithmetic_side_effects` violations along the way — it had never passed the lane's clippy gate.

- [x] Database Viewer (apps/dbviewer: SQL parser/executor for SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/CREATE/DROP, in-memory table storage with constraints, paginated data browser, column sorting, syntax-highlighted query editor, query history, CSV/JSON/SQL export, CSV import, WHERE clause builder, aggregate functions, schema diagram, multi-tab databases, multi-panel UI, 174 tests)
  - [x] **Windowed** (apps/dbviewer: `app::launch`, a `Layout` solved from the live window size every frame, and hit boxes recorded by the drawing pass). `main` built a sample database, drew one frame into a `Vec`, asserted the `Vec` was not empty and dropped it, so every control the pass painted — Execute, the database tabs and their close boxes, the object tree, the sorting column headers, the pagination arrows, the four bottom-panel tabs, the query history — was a rectangle that answered nothing. Wiring it found thirteen faults. **The layout went negative at small sizes**: `content_height`, `grid_height` and `main_width` all did, and because `SIDEBAR_WIDTH` was the only width the sidebar was ever drawn at, a 200-point window handed the grid a width of −20. **The toolbar started at a hard-coded `x = 130` and walked right without asking how wide the window was**, and so did the database tab strip. **Three passes tested the wrong edge or no edge at all**: the sidebar tree tested `ny > y + height` at the *top* of a row, so a row straddling the bottom edge was drawn whole; the filter builder under it tested nothing, so a database of a dozen tables pushed the only way to remove a filter off the end of the window; and the grid's row loop tested the same way and painted a 26-point band of row colour over the pagination bar. **Grid columns were drawn at a fixed 140 points and swallowed by the clip**, so a table that was being shown in part looked complete — the caption says how many of the columns are shown now. **The pagination caption was given `max_width: width - 200`**, negative in a narrow grid. **`delete_row` indexed the *table* while the screen showed filtered and sorted rows**, so deleting the third row on screen removed whatever was third in insertion order, silently and with a success message; `current_table_data` carries each row's source index now. **The schema pane's three columns were 180, 100 and 200 points wide whatever the pane measured**, so the constraints column was routinely drawn outside it and the clip made the table look unconstrained. **The diagram laid its table boxes in one unbounded row at `ti * 200`**, so the third table in a 400-point pane was drawn entirely outside it. **The status bar placed its three readings at `x + 10`, `x + 200` and `x + width - 150` whatever the window measured**, so a narrow window drew all three on top of each other. **Fourteen operations were reachable from nothing but the tests** — `toggle_sort`, `select_table`, `delete_row`, `remove_filter`, `add_filter`, `toggle_favorite`, the three exporters, the CSV importer, the three filter-builder fields and `bottom_panel` — and `export_current_table` returned a `String` nobody received. **Twelve crate-level `#![allow]`s** went with them, not one of which silenced anything the crate still trips. The reachability sweep then found the worst of them: **`draw_sql_editor` asked for an 80-point box for a pane 114 points tall**, of which 64 points were empty box because the editor draws a single unwrapped run — the first history row would have ended at 132, so *every* history row was past the bottom edge in every window there has ever been. `HISTORY (n queries)` was painted over nothing, and since `Frame::hit` drops a box outside the clip, no query could be recalled and no query could be starred: the two controls had never existed. The heading was rewritten with it, because the list is cut twice — to the five newest, then again by whatever is left of the pane — and a heading that only counted the queries let a pane showing three of twenty look like a pane showing all twenty. The suite is 174 tests, of which 25 are sweeps over twelve window sizes crossed with thirteen states, and every control test finds its control **by the words the picture shows** rather than by asking the frame where it thinks its own boxes are — a pass that recorded every row one row off would answer with the same offset it made the mistake with, so finding and clicking would cancel out and a grid where every row deleted its neighbour would pass. The mutation table is 49 rows. Sweeping it found one more fault, and it was a fault in the *test*: **a pass held only to its caller's box is not held to its own.** `draw_sidebar` splits its box into a tree section and a filter-builder section, and only `draw_sidebar` was in the pass list — so a tree row overrunning the tree section landed on top of the builder, which is still inside the sidebar and still looked correct from outside. `Pass` is a boxed closure now instead of a bare `fn` pointer, so `draw_object_tree` and `draw_filter_builder` — each handed a `DbTab` the alias could not express — are fenced against the boxes they are actually given. That still did not catch it, which exposed the second half: **a tree row fills only when it is the selected table**, so the fill-phrased assertion had nothing to witness. Fills are a *complete* witness (pushed exactly as asked under any clip), whereas runs and hit boxes are *one-way* ones (`put_text` refuses what the clip cannot show, `Frame::hit` drops what has nothing visible) — but this sweep hands each pass an unclipped frame, where nothing is suppressing them, so adding them can only add catches. With runs and hit boxes checked too it found a fourteenth production fault immediately: **three one-line placeholders — "No query results", "Select a table to view its schema", "No tables in database" — placed their line at a fixed inset from the top of the pane and inked it whatever the pane measured**, so a pane shorter than the inset got a line below its own bottom edge. In the window a clip tidies that away, which is exactly why it survived; they go through a `put_line` funnel that measures first now. And writing the table in the first place turned up two rules the suite asserted nowhere: **the pagination bar is measured from what is left under the column headers rather than taken off the bottom of the grid** (taken off the bottom, a grid too short for both puts the bar over its own header, the page count drawn on the column names and the arrows taking presses aimed at a sort), and **`render` was only ever checked to remember the size it was handed, not to draw at it** — a pass that drew at the size it was launched with would have passed every other test in the file, because every other test calls `frame` directly.

- [x] Regex Tester (apps/regextester: custom NFA regex engine with Thompson simulation, real-time match highlighting, capture groups display, find & replace with backreferences, regex pattern explanation, 18 built-in patterns across 6 categories, syntax reference, case-insensitive/global/multiline flags, pattern library, multi-panel UI, 75 tests)
- [x] File Diff/Compare (apps/filediff: Myers diff algorithm, side-by-side and unified diff views, inline character-level diff, color-coded changes, merge support with accept left/right/both, directory comparison mode, ignore whitespace/case/blank lines, search within diff, multi-panel UI)
- [x] Automation/Macro Recorder (apps/automator: **windowed** -- a macro library, an action list and a read-out flanking it, a transport strip of clickable buttons whose labels say what the keys do, an Editor/Script tab pair, speed and repeat pads, a help card, and a playback that advances on a clock; keyboard/mouse macro recording and playback, action types — key press/release, click, move, scroll, type text, wait; script language with variables and conditionals, configurable playback speed, repeat options, trigger system with hotkeys, macro library with import/export, recording indicator; 158 tests). Wiring it found sixteen faults on top of the missing window. (1) `main` built two demo macros, called `render`, bound the result to `let _commands` and returned -- the frame was rendered into a `Vec` and dropped on the next line, so nothing was ever shown and no click or key ever reached the program. (2) **Nothing was clickable, anywhere.** Not one control recorded a hit box, because there was nowhere for a click to come from: fourteen commands, five speed buttons, three repeat buttons, two tabs and every row of both lists were painted and unreachable. Hit boxes are recorded by the pass that paints them now. (3) **Every rectangle was a compile-time constant** -- `HEADER_HEIGHT = 44`, `TOOLBAR_HEIGHT = 40`, `SIDEBAR_WIDTH = 240`, `PROPERTIES_WIDTH = 260`. The centre panel was literally `width - 240.0 - 260.0`, **a negative width in any window narrower than five hundred pixels**, and the properties panel began at `width - 260.0`, which is off the left edge of anything narrower than that. Nothing measured anything vertical with the height at all. The layout is solved from the live size every frame now, and a side panel is taken only if it is wide enough to read *and* leaves the list wide enough to read. (4) **The speed and repeat pads were laid out from a fixed offset and ended up in the status bar**: the speed section was at `content_y + content_h - 100.0`, the repeat row 80 px below it and 24 px tall, so the pads finished **four pixels below the properties pane**, painted over the bar. They are a reserved strip of the panel now, taken out of it before the rows above are laid out. (5) **Both panel footers were drawn at `content_y + content_h - 36.0` unconditionally**, so in a short window the New/Delete bar was not under the library, it was on top of it -- and the rows it covered were drawn all the same, under a bar that hid them. One `Layout::split` now caps a footer at half of what the heading left, so a footer cannot eat the body it is the foot of. (6) **The two scroll offsets were written once and never again.** `sidebar_scroll` and `action_list_scroll` were `f32` fields assigned zero in `new()`, read by the drawing pass, and assigned nowhere else in the whole program: **a library with more macros than fit was permanently truncated at whatever the window could show**. They are `usize` row offsets driven by the wheel now, clamped to the content and made to follow the selection. (7) **`tick_playback` had no caller outside the tests**, so a macro started playing and then sat there: nothing in the program advanced it. `App::tick_interval` drives it now, and an idle Automator asks for no repaint rather than redrawing sixty times a second for ever. (8) **`active_tab` was assigned in exactly one place in the entire program, and that place was a test** -- the script editor could not be reached. The tab strip is drawn with hit boxes and Tab toggles it. (9) **Every command was reachable from a key or from a button, never both**, and eleven of the fourteen from neither. Each `Button` carries its own key and one `press` implementation, so the two cannot drift apart, and the button's face is what tells the user which key it is. (10) **Every string was `max_width: None`** -- macro names, action summaries, the status message, the header's playing read-out. On a narrow window they kept going past the edge. Every run goes through `bounded`/`centred` now, which refuse to draw into a box with no room left. (11) The header placed its indicators by guessing at its own text: the title at x 10, the recording dot at 110, `REC` at 128, and the playing read-out at either 110 or 175 -- **175 being a guess at how wide the word `REC` is**. Everything is measured now, and the tabs are laid out from the right edge inwards. (12) **Thirteen crate-level `#![allow]`s** -- `dead_code`, `unused_imports`, `too_many_lines`, four `cast_*` and the rest -- were hiding 62 real lints. All thirteen are gone. (13) **The scroll offsets followed the selection up but never down.** `clamp_scrolls` lowered an offset that sat above the selection and did nothing when the selection walked below the last visible row, so **holding <kbd>Down</kbd> walked the selection off the bottom of a list that did not move** -- and the arrow-key test, which only ever walked the selection upwards, was satisfied. Both offsets are held to `offset <= i < offset + rows` now, with the number of rows counted from the body the drawing pass actually uses. (14) **A click never put the offsets back in range.** The keys clamped and the buttons clamped; the click path was the one way in that did not, so clicking a forty-action macro, scrolling to the end of it and then clicking a one-action macro left the list scrolled thirty rows past its only row -- a blank panel with a selection in it. `click` is a wrapper that dispatches and then clamps now, so a target added later cannot forget (lesson 101). The suite sweeps a 48-point grid of window sizes for every layout invariant (lesson 97) and draws eight states rather than one for every whole-frame claim (lesson 99), and it earned that width immediately: it found the list being charged for a side panel that had been *dropped* for want of height, leaving a strip of window belonging to no pane at all; an action row laying its four columns left to right and bounding only the last of them, so the row number and the type badge were painted past the row's own right edge; the script tab's line-number gutter being three digits wide however narrow the text area, so the numbers went off the edge and the lines had nowhere left; and an empty library drawing a blank body under a heading reading `Macros (0)`, which is indistinguishable from a panel that failed to draw. A 62-row `apps/automator/mutate.py` proves each test is the one that catches its own fault, and the first sweep of it was worth the forty minutes it took: twelve rows survived, and not one of them was answered by tightening an assertion until it passed. Two were faults 13 and 14 above. Four were the price of a hit-box invariant I had restated in terms of paint order -- `Frame::hit_test` returns the last-painted match by construction, so the restatement could not fail, and a hit box moved away from the ink it belongs to walked through it; the claim is split in two now, one test tying every hit box to a `FillRect` at the same rectangle and one holding it inside the band of the pane that owns it (lesson 100). Two were mutations of guards that cannot be load-bearing, and the rows were replaced rather than the tests: an empty `Rect` makes every `fill` and `bounded` downstream refuse of its own accord, and `Frame::hit` drops an empty box, so striking either guard out changes no picture. The rest were rows whose mutation did not do what the row's name said -- a "runs off the right-hand edge" that moved the panel left into an overlap instead. (15) **Fault 7 was fixed by half, and lane B caught it with a gate five days later** (`requests/b-c-automator-never-receives-the-clock.md`). `tick` was written and `App::tick_interval` asked for a clock, but `handle_event` never grew an `Event::Tick` arm, so every tick the compositor delivered fell into `_ => EventResult::Ignored` and **nothing in the running program ever called `tick` at all**. The window still laid out, still repainted and still answered the keyboard, so nothing looked wrong -- while a macro that was "playing" sat on its first action for the life of the process, the recording indicator never pulsed, and `elapsed_ms` read a plausible zero. Four rows of the mutation table break `tick` and **all four were caught**, by tests that call `tick` directly against a program in which `tick` was unreachable: the table proved the worker and never knocked on the door. It broke `scripts/check-tick-wiring.py`, which is the boot test's *first* step, so for a day lane C's app was blocking all three lanes from merging -- lane A crossed into `apps/**` under the red-trunk exception and landed the arm on `main` as `f64ad6259`, correctly, nineteen minutes before this branch's version was finished; the merge conflicted and was resolved in favour of this one, which is A's single test split three ways plus two more (`requests/a-c-automator-asked-for-a-clock-and-threw-it-away-i-fixed-it-on-main.md` §6). The arm is in, and four of the five new tests enter through `handle_event`; the new mutation row deletes the arm rather than the body, and is caught by four tests, none of them the two that guarded `tick` before (lesson 102). (16) **The wall clock and the age of the window were the same field**, which the arm would have turned from harmless into wrong. `Macro::created_at_ms`/`modified_at_ms` are dates; the clock the window delivers is an *interval*, and the nine stamp sites were fed from the accumulated `self.elapsed_ms`. Frozen, that read zero everywhere; live, **every macro would have claimed to be created a few seconds after 1970-01-01**. There is a separate `wall_ms` now, seeded and re-read from a `now_ms()` that asks `SystemTime` (re-read on the tick, so a macro made an hour into a session is not stamped with the hour the program started), while the `recording_last_event_ms` sites stay on `elapsed_ms` -- they measure the gap between two recorded events, which is what an interval is for. The table is 67 rows.) (17) **Six more, found by giving the program its first per-pass containment test.** Everything above is bounded by the *window*; nothing was bounded by the *pane it belongs to*, so a pass that overran its own box by a few points painted on a sibling and every one of the 157 tests was satisfied. Five of the six faults are one mistake written five ways: a run placed by **centring** it in a strip, with nothing checking the strip is as tall as the line. `band.y + (band.h - size) / 2.0` is above `band.y` the moment the strip is shorter than the line and hangs the same distance below it, and every heading, footer button and status bar has a window size at which it is squeezed that far. The horizontal form of this was already fixed here -- `centred` clamps its offset at zero, and there is a mutation row for it -- and the vertical form went unseen because **both window-level text tests bound a run's left and right edges and neither binds its top and bottom**: a run has no height in the command stream, so you have to supply `font_size` as its height before the question can be asked at all. One helper, `centre_line(band, size) -> Option<f32>`, now stands between all eighteen call sites and the arithmetic -- not eighteen copies of the comparison, which is how a rule comes to hold in seventeen places and not the eighteenth. Two variants the helper does not cover were fixed in their own terms: the header's recording dot is a **fill** of a literal size centred in the header, and shrinks to it (`dot.min(head.h)`) rather than being centred in something smaller than itself; and `draw_pads` split its strip into quarters as bare *offsets*, with no band for a helper to be handed, so the four quarters are named as `Rect`s now. The sixth fault needed the test to go further than the layout does. The script tab's error strip was hung off the bottom of its **text area** instead of the bottom of its **body**; the two agree exactly until the text area's height clamps at zero, which needs a body under about seven points tall, and **no window in the 48-size grid splits the list into a body that short** -- so the fault sat in the tree with a sweep over it that could not reach it, and its mutation row survived. A sub-pass takes its box as an argument, which means the test can hand it one: `squeezes(r)` yields `r` plus `r` at heights `[0, 1, 3, 6, 12]` and widths `[1, 5, 30]`, and every Rect-taking pass is swept over all of them. That caught the strip on the first run and then found two more on the next two: the pads' headings above, and an action row's playing marker, a fill three literal points wide -- the one thing in a row that no other measurement bounds. A seventh sample was added to the height grid for the same reason: `[0, 18, 55, ...]` jumps from a box every pass refuses to a box everything fits in, and the band between them, where a strip exists but cannot show anything, is where the recording dot's fault lived. The suite is 158 tests and the table 73 rows (lesson 109).)
- [x] Code Snippets Manager (apps/snippets: **windowed** — three columns under a toolbar, every band, column and row height solved from the live window size each frame: a sidebar of six views (folders as an expandable tree, tags, languages, favourites, recently used, templates), a list of what matched under a sort header, and the one you picked shown with syntax highlighting for twelve languages, template placeholders, a JSON export written to a file, search over names/content/tags under three scopes, and a stats overlay; 169 tests). Wiring it found sixteen faults, the missing window among them. (1) **`main` was `let app = App::new(); let _cmds = app.render();`** — it built the library, drew one frame into a `Vec`, dropped it and returned, so no frame ever reached a screen and no key or click ever arrived. (2) **The program invented its own window and then drew in absolute pixels inside it.** `render` took no size at all; all five drawing passes were placed against `WINDOW_WIDTH`, `WINDOW_HEIGHT`, `SIDEBAR_WIDTH = 200`, `LIST_WIDTH = 280` and `TOOLBAR_HEIGHT = 44`. A window the user made narrower did not narrow the columns, it cut the editor off; a taller one left a band of nothing under all three. (3) **There was no input of any kind** — no `handle_event`, no key arm, no mouse arm, not one line in the file that could change the model. Nine fields of view state (`sidebar_view`, `sort_order`, `search_query`, `search_scope`, `selected_snippet_id`, `selected_folder_id`, `show_stats`, `list_scroll`, `scroll_offset`) were read by the drawing pass and written by nothing at all: the whole program was a picture of one fixed state. (4) **The toolbar drew its buttons on top of its own search box.** The four buttons started at a hardcoded `bx = 180.0` and each grew by its measured label; the search box began at `WINDOW_WIDTH - 320.0`; nothing checked the first stopped before the second, and the title's `max_width` was a fixed `160.0` chosen to match the 180 by eye. The row measures itself and drops what will not fit, right to left, now. (5) **Two of the sidebar's lists had holes in them.** A folder's row went at `items_y + fi * 26.0` where `fi` counts *every* folder including the nested ones the loop skips, so one nested folder left a blank row where the next top-level one belonged; the language list had the same fault with the languages it skips for having no snippets, which is most of them — twelve languages, three in use, drawn at rows 0, 4 and 9. Nested folders are drawn as a tree under their parent now, which is what `Folder::expanded` — set to `true` in three places and read nowhere — was always for. (6) **The snippet list drew through its own header and out of the bottom of the window.** A row was skipped only when a full row above the top or entirely below `WINDOW_HEIGHT`, so a row scrolled up by half its height drew over the sort header and the last row drew past the window edge on to whatever came next. Nothing clipped. Both panels clip to their own rectangle now and `guitk::scroll_window` decides which rows are in them. (7) **Both scroll offsets were pixel counts into things made of rows** — the code panel divided `scroll_offset` by a line height to get a line number, the list subtracted `list_scroll` from a row's `y` — so both could express positions the renderer then rounded away, and neither had an upper bound, because neither was ever assigned. Both are row indices clamped to the last page now. (8) **`use_snippet` recorded uses of snippets that do not exist.** The `if let` that bumps the count quietly finds nothing for an unknown id, and the `recently_used.insert(0, id)` beneath it ran regardless — so an id that was never a snippet took one of the twenty places in the recent list and pushed a real entry off the end. (9) **`create_snippet` and `create_folder` said "no" by returning `0`,** which is the same `u64` a real id is; nothing distinguished a refused create from one that made snippet zero except knowing `IdGen` starts at one. Both return an `Option` now. (10) **The toolbar had an Import button for a feature the program does not have.** There is no import function in the file and never was; the module doc claimed "Import/export (JSON format)" and only the export half was ever written. Export writes the JSON to a file and says on the status line whether it worked; Import is gone until there is something behind it. (11) **Seventeen lints were blanket-allowed in sixteen `#![allow(...)]` at the top of the file**, `dead_code` among them — which is what let a program whose `main` discards its own render compile without a word of complaint, along with the six `edit_*` fields and the `editing` flag of an editor that could not be entered, `modified_at` (set to the same value as `created_at` at the one site that sets either, then never read, never exported and never updated, because nothing in the program can modify a snippet), and `apply_template`, which nothing but a test has ever called. Three more faults surfaced only once the tests were written, and the code was what was wrong each time. (12) **The seeded library was flat** — three folders, none inside another — so `Folder::parent_id`, the indent in `draw_folder_tree`, the twisty and the recursion in `walk_folders` were all written for nesting that nothing a user opened the program on could produce; a `Regex` folder sits inside `Utilities` now, and the twisty tests that first panicked with "the seeded library has a folder with children" exercise it. (13) **Home and End each did the other's job on a fresh window.** Both were routed through `move_selection(isize::MIN)` and `isize::MAX`, and that function's no-selection branch reads the *sign* of the delta to decide which end a walk enters the list from — so End, a positive delta, entered at the top and Home at the bottom. They are absolute movements, not very large relative ones, and go through `select_end` now. (14) **`label_centred` centred on the measured width and told the renderer to stop at the box width,** so a label offset into the middle of its box was allowed to run half the leftover slack past the box's right edge; its own doc comment claimed the two widths could not disagree. The empty editor's headline, centred in the whole column, reached 229 pixels outside a 1100-wide window on that arithmetic. The last two came out of the mutation sweep, which broke the production code 128 ways and caught 113 of them first time. (15) **The folder walk carried a depth cap put in against a folder that is its own ancestor, and the cap could not do that job.** A folder has one parent and the walk enters from `None`, so a folder is reached only if its parent chain ends at `None` and every folder in a cycle has a chain that never does — the cycle is a separate component the walk never enters, and the same argument bounds the recursion at `folders.len()`. What the cap actually did was truncate: a ninth level of nesting, which the New Folder button will build since it files the new folder under whichever one is picked, vanished from the tree with the snippets in it. The test named for it built a two-folder cycle that the walk was never going to reach, so it passed with the cap set to `usize::MAX`. (16) **`move_selection` refused an empty list twice,** once in a `checked_sub(1)` early return and once in the `ids.get(next)` beneath it that has to be there anyway; with no rows the first is unreachable and no test could tell it from its own absence (`known-issues.md` lesson 51). Its neighbour `.min(last)` looks equally redundant against that same `get` and is not: it is the only thing that fetches a selected row back on screen when a walk runs off the end (lesson 70), which is a case no test had. Four of the survivors were tests that could not see what they were named for — one pressed End, which reaches the row through `select_end` and its own into-view call rather than through the walk being tested; one used a window the header still fitted in, so none of the clamps below it ran; one walked `stat_rows`, the very list the drawing walks, and so asked the code whether it drew what it drew; one clicked a toolbar button laid out from the left edge, which shifts by less than its own width between the two window sizes, so the click landed on the old box either way and the resize could be thrown away unnoticed. Three toolkit gaps came out of the same sweep: `Probe` had no way to deliver a wheel event, so every wheel test called the app's router directly and supplied the answer it was meant to be checking — the list's hit box could be deleted outright with all four passing (`Probe::scroll_at` and `probe::scroll_at_point` now, and the box is reached where it is actually reachable, in the strip below the last row that `scroll_window::capacity`'s floor leaves over); `probe::press` had no counterpart for the key coming back up, so no suite could tell a program that ignores the release from one that runs every shortcut twice; and `mutation_harness` would start a compiler on a table whose anchors do not appear exactly once, and treated an empty expectation as satisfied by anything, since `set() <= failed` holds for every failure set. Window-wiring baseline 73 -> 72.
